Class-level workflow for 'unzip this and run the plan' handoffs (ACMS, llm-manager-project-framework, future frameworks).\"\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Packages, Handoffs, PRs, Validation, agents-md]\n    related_skills: [github-pr-workflow, deployed-service-capture, github-repo-management]\n---\n\n# Execute a Delivered Implementation Package (zip + instructions → GitHub)\n\nUse when Jordan sends a zip (repo content / overlay trees) plus an instruction markdown (\"UPLOAD_AND_EXECUTE_…\", runbook, or plan) targeting a GitHub repo. The instruction file is the authoritative spec — follow its steps literally, **including its STOP/human-review gates** (e.g. \"Do not merge PR-001 autonomously\", \"human must approve ADR-0007 before merge\"). Two occurrences so far: LLM-Manager docs package and ACMS bootstrap + PR-001 (`startupteams/acms-project-framework`, 2026-09-25); both share the shape: one docs tree for `main`, one code overlay for a feature branch + PR.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **Extract & inventory.** `unzip` may not be installed — fall back to `python3 -c \"import zipfile; zipfile.ZipFile(p).extractall(d)\"`. List all files in every tree; read the instruction MD fully before acting.\n2. **Secret scan the whole package BEFORE any commit** (same rule as `deployed-service-capture`): grep for `sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}`, DSNs `://…@`, `ghp_`/`github_pat_`, private-key headers, password/api-key lines. If the target is under `startupteams` (or any org outside `jordatech`), get **explicit per-task authorization via clarify** before pushing — user-profile boundary. A direct-to-`main` bootstrap commit is allowed ONLY if the instruction file states it's human-authorized (one-time exception); all later feature work goes through branch + PR.\n4. **Clone fresh** to `~/work/<repo>` (don't reuse stale clones). Apply each tree per the plan's `cp -a \"<tree>/.\" \"$REPO_DIR/\"`, stage **only the file lists the plan specifies**, commit with the plan's message, push.\n5. **Re-run validation in the live repo before opening the PR**, even if the plan says validation already passed on the prepared overlay. Match the project's `requires-python` (check `pyproject.toml`) — system python may be older; create a venv with the right interpreter (`/usr/bin/python3.12 -m venv .venv-<name>`), `pip install -e '.[dev]'`, run the test command from the plan.\n6. **Artifact-cleanliness gate before staging** (see Pitfalls).\n7. **Open the PR** with `gh pr create --body-file <package>/PR_BODY.md` — keep the PR body as a file in the extract dir, do NOT commit `PR_BODY.md` into the repo. Verify with `gh pr view <n> --json state,baseRefName,headRefName,additions,fileCount`.\n8. **Stop at the plan's human gate.** Never self-merge. State clearly in the final reply which decision is waiting on Jordan (e.g. approve/modify a specific ADR).\n9. **Log the milestone to `~/agents.md`** (repo, commits, PR link + number, gate status, any fixes made during execution, local paths) and update memory only with durable facts (repo authorization, clone location) — not PR numbers/commit SHAs as standalone memory entries; agents.md is the log.\n\n## Continuing/revising an OPEN PR across sessions\n\nWhen a plan says \"revise the existing PR, do not open a new one\" (e.g. the ACMS PR #1 async-stack revision after ADR-0007 acceptance):\n\n1. Re-derive state from the live repo first: `git status`, `git log --oneline`, `gh pr view N --json state,updatedAt`, and re-read the plan's stop conditions. Prior-session work may sit uncommitted in the working tree — validate it, don't redo it.\n2. Re-run the FULL validation suite in the live repo before committing (tests, stack-validation script, compile check, secret scan) — never trust the previous session's \"it passed\".\n3. Commit as focused follow-up commits on the same branch (`feat(...)` / `test(...)` / `docs(...)` split), push, and **update the PR body** to reflect the revision (see pitfall below — `gh pr edit` fails silently on this). Merge stays a human action.\n4. Write a new dated handoff doc in the repo (`docs/handoffs/<date>-<slug>.md`) with a validation table of exact executed commands + results, commit it on the branch, attach the path in the final reply.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- **`gh pr edit --body-file` silently fails on orgs with projects-classic**: errors with a \"Projects (classic) is being deprecated\" GraphQL message, exits non-zero, and the body is NOT updated. Use the REST fallback `gh api -X PATCH repos/OWNER/REPO/pulls/N --input -` (JSON `{\"body\": ...}` on stdin), then VERIFY with `gh pr view N --json body -q .body | grep -c \"<unique-marker>\"`.\n- **`&&` chains abort on a no-match grep.** `grep -c pattern file && git add ...` returns rc=1 when the count is 0, silently skipping the rest of the chain (a commit+push never ran and looked \"done\"). Run commits/pushes as their own commands, or append `|| true` to informational greps.\n- **Foreground terminal refuses inline scripts that spawn a server** (\"appears to start a long-lived server/watch process\") even when the script self-terminates. Write the script to a file (e.g. `scripts/validate_*.py`) and run it with `terminal(background=true, notify_on_complete=true)`; keep it printing a clear PASS line.\n- **No Docker needed to validate a real PostgreSQL stack**: the `pgserver` dev extra runs embedded PostgreSQL 16 — `pgserver.get_server(dir, cleanup_mode=\"delete\")` gives a URI for Alembic-upgrade-from-zero, uvicorn-subprocess HTTP checks, and after-exit durability queries. Recipe: `references/pg-stack-validation-no-docker.md`. Do NOT record \"docker is unavailable\" as an environment constraint; use this path.\n- **Accidental branch creation from defensive commands**: a guard like `git checkout -b <branch>-tmp 2>/dev/null` succeeds and leaves you on the temp branch with the same content — verify `git branch --show-current` before committing, and delete stray temp branches.\n\n- **Build artifacts sneak into `git add` of directories.** Running pytest/pip-install inside the repo creates `__pycache__/`, `*.egg-info/`, `.venv*/`, local `*.db`; `git add acms tests` sweeps the .pyc files in even when the plan lists exact paths. Before committing: `git status --short --ignored`, check `.gitignore` covers `__pycache__/`, `*.py[cod]`, `.venv*/`, `*.egg-info/`, `*.db`; extend it (with `patch`/append, never overwrite) or delete artifacts first. If a commit already caught them: `git rm -r --cached <dirs>` + extend `.gitignore` + follow-up `chore:` commit on the same PR branch (don't rewrite pushed history).\n- **Instruction scripts assume `unzip`, a writable `$HOME/work`, and exact python availability** — substitute equivalents (python zipfile, explicit REPO_DIR, versioned interpreter + venv) rather than failing.\n- **Plan file-lists omit generated files you create while validating** (venv, egg-info from `pip install -e`). If `gh pr create` warns \"uncommitted change\", check whether it's untracked local junk (gitignore it) vs. genuinely missing content.\n- **Don't apply the whole package blindly at repo root** — the instruction MD itself and `PR_BODY.md` belong outside the tree (the plan's `git add` lists show exactly what enters the repo).\n- Overlay trees may MODIFY files already on `main` (bootstrap case): `git status` after `cp -a` shows ` M` entries — that's expected; still stage only the plan's listed paths.\n\n## Related\n\n- `deployed-service-capture` — sibling direction (live VM → repo); shares the two-pass secret-scan rule and `references/secret-scan-patterns.md` patterns there.\n- `github-pr-workflow` — generic PR lifecycle commands (gh and curl fallbacks).\n- `github-repo-management` — clone/remote basics.\n", "path": "devops/implementation-package-execution/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/implementation-package-execution", "linked_files": null, "usage_hint": null, "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["GitHub", "Packages", "Handoffs", "PRs", "Validation", "agents-md"], "related_skills": ["github-pr-workflow", "deployed-service-capture", "github-repo-management"]}}}

{"success": true, "name": "github-repo-management", "description": "Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Repositories", "Git", "Releases", "Secrets", "Configuration"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-pr-workflow", "github-issues"], "content": "---\nname: github-repo-management\ndescription: \"Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Repositories, Git, Releases, Secrets, Configuration]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-pr-workflow, github-issues]\n---\n\n# GitHub Repository Management\n\nCreate, clone, fork, configure, and manage GitHub repositories. Each section shows `gh` first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n- **Troubleshooting GitHub CLI Authentication**: If `gh auth status` fails despite having `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported, try:\n  1. Unset the environment variable: `unset GITHUB_TOKEN`\n  2. Run `gh auth login` and authenticate via browser or paste token when prompted\n  3. Alternatively, use direct curl commands with `-H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\"` for API calls\n  4. Note that `gh` commands may not always pick up `GITHUB_TOKEN` from environment in some contexts\n- **Important**: Always verify authentication before proceeding. If `gh auth status` fails, run `gh auth login` to authenticate.\n\n### Setup\n\n```bash\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if [ -f ~/.hermes/.env ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" ~/.hermes/.env; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" ~/.hermes/.env | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\\\([^@]*\\\\)@.*|\\\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\n\n# Get your GitHub username (needed for several operations)\nif [ \"$AUTH\" = \"gh\" ]; then\n  GH_USER=$(gh api user --jq '.login')\nelse\n  GH_USER=$(curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" https://api.github.com/user | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['login'])\")\nfi\n```\n\nIf you're inside a repo already:\n\n```bash\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. Cloning Repositories\n\nCloning is pure `git` — works identically either way:\n\n```bash\n# Clone via HTTPS (works with credential helper or token-embedded URL)\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone into a specific directory\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git ./my-local-dir\n\n# Shallow clone (faster for large repos)\ngit clone --depth 1 https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone a specific branch\ngit clone --branch develop https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone via SSH (if SSH is configured)\ngit clone git@github.com:owner/repo-name.git\n```\n\n**With gh (shorthand):**\n\n```bash\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name -- --depth 1\n```\n\n## SOUL.md / Agent Profile Resource Repositories\n\nWhen a Hermes profile `SOUL.md` or agent identity file lists resource repositories to consult or edit, treat it as a repository-management task:\n\n- Read the active profile's `SOUL.md` first and extract bot name, repo URLs, allowed GitHub org/owner boundaries, Git identity, and branch rules.\n- Use a stable profile-local workspace such as `~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/resource_repositories/`.\n- Convert GitHub `/tree/<branch>/<path>` URLs to clone URLs, clone/fetch each repo, and check out the bot-name branch (for example `c01entrepreneur_bot`) when present.\n- If `origin/<bot_name>` does not exist, create a local bot-name branch from the remote default branch.\n- Verify origin URL, branch, upstream, HEAD, and dirty status before reporting completion.\n- If a referenced `github.com/<org>/<repo>` returns API `404` or HTTPS clone requests credentials, report it as likely private/inaccessible and ask for GitHub auth/access rather than guessing an alternate source.\n- For the entrepreneur profile/Jordan work, treat the allowed GitHub owner boundary as authoritative: use only `jordatech/*` repositories unless the user explicitly authorizes another owner in the current turn. If the user links a non-`jordatech` repo by mistake and then corrects it, stop using that source, fetch the equivalent `jordatech` source, and verify the remote URL before checkout/copy/commit.\n- In Hermes profile sandboxes, a repo can appear unauthenticated even when `gh auth status` works from the real user home. For `git fetch`, `git pull --rebase`, `git push`, and `git ls-remote`, prefer `HOME=/home/$USER` (and `GH_CONFIG_DIR=/home/$USER/.config/gh` for `gh` if needed). `gh auth setup-git` alone may not fix raw `git` commands when the active profile `HOME` is different from the real user home.\n\nSee `references/soul-resource-repositories.md` for the reusable checkout/sync script pattern, verification checklist, exact-match SOUL sync pitfalls, and pitfalls from the entrepreneur profile setup, including the Hermes profile-HOME vs real-user-HOME GitHub auth quirk.\n\nSee `references/github-auth-troubleshooting.md` for troubleshooting GitHub CLI authentication issues when environment variables are not picked up correctly.\n\nSee `references/noninteractive-fork-import-and-git-auth.md` for the fallback pattern when GitHub forking is disabled, `jordatech` is a user account rather than an org, or HTTPS git pushes fail in non-interactive sessions despite valid `gh` auth.\n\n## 2. Creating Repositories\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# Create a public repo and clone it\ngh repo create my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# Private, with description and license\ngh repo create my-new-project --private --description \"A useful tool\" --license MIT --clone\n\n# Under an organization\ngh repo create my-org/my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# From existing local directory\ncd /path/to/existing/project\ngh repo create my-project --source . --public --push\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create the remote repo via API\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/user/repos \\\n  -d '{\n    \"name\": \"my-new-project\",\n    \"description\": \"A useful tool\",\n    \"private\": false,\n    \"auto_init\": true,\n    \"license_template\": \"mit\"\n  }'\n\n# Clone it\ngit clone https://github.com/$GH_USER/my-new-project.git\ncd my-new-project\n\n# -- OR -- push an existing local directory to the new repo\ncd /path/to/existing/project\ngit init\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"Initial commit\"\ngit remote add origin https://github.com/$GH_USER/my-new-project.git\ngit push -u origin main\n```\n\nTo create under an organization:\n\n```bash\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/orgs/my-org/repos \\\n  -d '{\"name\": \"my-new-project\", \"private\": false}'\n```\n\n### From a Template\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo create my-new-app --template owner/template-repo --public --clone\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/owner/template-repo/generate \\\n  -d '{\"owner\": \"'\"$GH_USER\"'\", \"name\": \"my-new-app\", \"private\": false}'\n```\n\n## 3. Forking Repositories\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo fork owner/repo-name --clone\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create the fork via API\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/owner/repo-name/forks\n\n# Wait a moment for GitHub to create it, then clone\nsleep 3\ngit clone https://github.com/$GH_USER/repo-name.git\ncd repo-name\n\n# Add the original repo as \"upstream\" remote\ngit remote add upstream https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n```\n\n### Keeping a Fork in Sync\n\n```bash\n# Pure git — works everywhere\ngit fetch upstream\ngit checkout main\ngit merge upstream/main\ngit push origin main\n```\n\n**With gh (shortcut):**\n\n```bash\ngh repo sync $GH_USER/repo-name\n```\n\n## 4. Repository Information\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo view owner/repo-name\ngh repo list --limit 20\ngh search repos \"machine learning\" --language python --sort stars\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# View repo details\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nr = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Name: {r['full_name']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Description: {r['description']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Stars: {r['stargazers_count']}  Forks: {r['forks_count']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Default branch: {r['default_branch']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Language: {r['language']}\\\")\"\n\n# List your repos\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/user/repos?per_page=20&sort=updated\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    vis = 'private' if r['private'] else 'public'\n    print(f\\\"  {r['full_name']:40}  {vis:8}  {r.get('language', ''):10}  ★{r['stargazers_count']}\\\")\"\n\n# Search repos\ncurl -s \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/search/repositories?q=machine+learning+language:python&sort=stars&per_page=10\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin)['items']:\n    print(f\\\"  {r['full_name']:40}  ★{r['stargazers_count']:6}  {r['description'][:60] if r['description'] else ''}\\\")\"\n```\n\n## 5. Repository Settings\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo edit --description \"Updated description\" --visibility public\ngh repo edit --enable-wiki=false --enable-issues=true\ngh repo edit --default-branch main\ngh repo edit --add-topic \"machine-learning,python\"\ngh repo edit --enable-auto-merge\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\ncurl -s -X PATCH \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO \\\n  -d '{\n    \"description\": \"Updated description\",\n    \"has_wiki\": false,\n    \"has_issues\": true,\n    \"allow_auto_merge\": true\n  }'\n\n# Update topics\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.mercy-preview+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/topics \\\n  -d '{\"names\": [\"machine-learning\", \"python\", \"automation\"]}'\n```\n\n## 6. Branch Protection\n\n```bash\n# View current protection\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/branches/main/protection\n\n# Set up branch protection\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/branches/main/protection \\\n  -d '{\n    \"required_status_checks\": {\n      \"strict\": true,\n      \"contexts\": [\"ci/test\", \"ci/lint\"]\n    },\n    \"enforce_admins\": false,\n    \"required_pull_request_reviews\": {\n      \"required_approving_review_count\": 1\n    },\n    \"restrictions\": null\n  }'\n```\n\n## 7. Secrets Management (GitHub Actions)\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh secret set API_KEY --body \"your-secret-value\"\ngh secret set SSH_KEY < ~/.ssh/id_rsa\ngh secret list\ngh secret delete API_KEY\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\nSecrets require encryption with the repo's public key — more involved via API:\n\n```bash\n# Get the repo's public key for encrypting secrets\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/secrets/public-key\n\n# Encrypt and set (requires Python with PyNaCl)\npython3 -c \"\nfrom base64 import b64encode\nfrom nacl import encoding, public\nimport json, sys\n\n# Get the public key\nkey_id = '<key_id_from_above>'\npublic_key = '<base64_key_from_above>'\n\n# Encrypt\nsealed = public.SealedBox(\n    public.PublicKey(public_key.encode('utf-8'), encoding.Base64Encoder)\n).encrypt('your-secret-value'.encode('utf-8'))\nprint(json.dumps({\n    'encrypted_value': b64encode(sealed).decode('utf-8'),\n    'key_id': key_id\n}))\"\n\n# Then PUT the encrypted secret\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/secrets/API_KEY \\\n  -d '<output from python script above>'\n\n# List secrets (names only, values hidden)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/secrets \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor s in json.load(sys.stdin)['secrets']:\n    print(f\\\"  {s['name']:30}  updated: {s['updated_at']}\\\")\"\n```\n\nNote: For secrets, `gh secret set` is dramatically simpler. If setting secrets is needed and `gh` isn't available, recommend installing it for just that operation.\n\n## 8. Releases\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh release create v1.0.0 --title \"v1.0.0\" --generate-notes\ngh release create v2.0.0-rc1 --draft --prerelease --generate-notes\ngh release create v1.0.0 ./dist/binary --title \"v1.0.0\" --notes \"Release notes\"\ngh release list\ngh release download v1.0.0 --dir ./downloads\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create a release\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/releases \\\n  -d '{\n    \"tag_name\": \"v1.0.0\",\n    \"name\": \"v1.0.0\",\n    \"body\": \"## Changelog\\n- Feature A\\n- Bug fix B\",\n    \"draft\": false,\n    \"prerelease\": false,\n    \"generate_release_notes\": true\n  }'\n\n# List releases\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/releases \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    tag = r.get('tag_name', 'no tag')\n    print(f\\\"  {tag:15}  {r['name']:30}  {'draft' if r['draft'] else 'published'}\\\")\"\n\n# Upload a release asset (binary file)\nRELEASE_ID=<id_from_create_response>\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/octet-stream\" \\\n  \"https://uploads.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/releases/$RELEASE_ID/assets?name=binary-amd64\" \\\n  --data-binary @./dist/binary-amd64\n```\n\n## 9. GitHub Actions Workflows\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh workflow list\ngh run list --limit 10\ngh run view <RUN_ID>\ngh run view <RUN_ID> --log-failed\ngh run rerun <RUN_ID>\ngh run rerun <RUN_ID> --failed\ngh workflow run ci.yml --ref main\ngh workflow run deploy.yml -f environment=staging\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# List workflows\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/workflows \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor w in json.load(sys.stdin)['workflows']:\n    print(f\\\"  {w['id']:10}  {w['name']:30}  {w['state']}\\\")\"\n\n# List recent runs\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs?per_page=10\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin)['workflow_runs']:\n    print(f\\\"  Run {r['id']}  {r['name']:30}  {r['conclusion'] or r['status']}\\\")\"\n\n# Download failed run logs\nRUN_ID=<run_id>\ncurl -s -L \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/logs \\\n  -o /tmp/ci-logs.zip\ncd /tmp && unzip -o ci-logs.zip -d ci-logs\n\n# Re-run a failed workflow\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/rerun\n\n# Re-run only failed jobs\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/rerun-failed-jobs\n\n# Trigger a workflow manually (workflow_dispatch)\nWORKFLOW_ID=<workflow_id_or_filename>\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/workflows/$WORKFLOW_ID/dispatches \\\n  -d '{\"ref\": \"main\", \"inputs\": {\"environment\": \"staging\"}}'\n```\n\n## 10. Gists\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh gist create script.py --public --desc \"Useful script\"\ngh gist list\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create a gist\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/gists \\\n  -d '{\n    \"description\": \"Useful script\",\n    \"public\": true,\n    \"files\": {\n      \"script.py\": {\"content\": \"print(\\\"hello\\\")\"}\n    }\n  }'\n\n# List your gists\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/gists \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor g in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    files = ', '.join(g['files'].keys())\n    print(f\\\"  {g['id']}  {g['description'] or '(no desc)':40}  {files}\\\")\"\n```\n\n## Quick Reference Table\n\n| Action | gh | git + curl |\n|--------|-----|-----------|\n| Clone | `gh repo clone o/r` | `git clone https://github.com/o/r.git` |\n| Create repo | `gh repo create name --public` | `curl POST /user/repos` |\n| Fork | `gh repo fork o/r --clone` | `curl POST /repos/o/r/forks` + `git clone` |\n| Repo info | `gh repo view o/r` | `curl GET /repos/o/r` |\n| Edit settings | `gh repo edit --...` | `curl PATCH /repos/o/r` |\n| Create release | `gh release create v1.0` | `curl POST /repos/o/r/releases` |\n| List workflows | `gh workflow list` | `curl GET /repos/o/r/actions/workflows` |\n| Rerun CI | `gh run rerun ID` | `curl POST /repos/o/r/actions/runs/ID/rerun` |\n| Set secret | `gh secret set KEY` | `curl PUT /repos/o/r/actions/secrets/KEY` (+ encryption) |\n", "path": # PDU Manager Live-Service Capture and GitHub Commit Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Target repository:** `https://github.com/startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`  
**Production host:** Proxmox node `MIAM-00133`  
**Production guest:** VM `154`  
**Current web UI:** `https://10.0.20.154/`  
**Execution mode:** Autonomous discovery and capture, with explicit stop gates for secrets, destructive actions, significant architecture changes, and merge/production deployment.

## 1. Mission

Capture the complete, currently running PDU Manager service from VM154 into the GitHub repository so that the repository becomes an understandable, reviewable, reproducible source of truth.

The agent must capture enough information to answer all of the following from GitHub alone:

1. What the PDU Manager is for and why the business operates it.
2. What code actually serves `https://10.0.20.154/`.
3. How the service is started and kept running.
4. What runtime, packages, services, files, configuration, certificates, and external integrations it depends on.
5. Which configuration is safe to commit and which values are secrets or site-local state.
6. How a clean VM can be made to host the same service.
7. How to validate the service without turning any PDU outlet on, off, or rebooting it.
8. Which behavior is implemented today and which behavior is only planned.

The final code capture must be committed on a branch and presented for human review. **Do not merge the branch autonomously.**

## 2. Business reason

The PDU Manager is operational infrastructure for the MARION-IA-USA server environment. It centralizes remote visibility and controlled power operations for rack equipment connected to network-managed PDUs. Capturing the service in GitHub is required because a service that exists only as mutable files on a production VM is difficult to reproduce, review, test, recover, or safely modify.

Moving the source into GitHub provides:

- disaster-recovery capability if VM154 is lost;
- traceable changes to power-control logic and outlet metadata;
- human review before production changes;
- reproducible deployment instead of one-off shell edits;
- automated validation and secret scanning;
- a clear handoff point for AI agents and humans;
- a safe foundation for CI/CD;
- protection against accidental configuration drift between documentation, source, and the running service.

## 3. Known production context to preserve and verify

Treat these as **known environment facts / intended behavior that must be verified against the live VM**, not as permission to invent implementation details.

- The PDU Manager runs on VM154 hosted by Proxmox node `MIAM-00133`.
- The user reaches the service at `https://10.0.20.154/`.
- The environment contains three network-managed PDU endpoints at `10.0.20.151`, `10.0.20.152`, and `10.0.20.153`.
- The UI currently identifies the backend as a direct SSH / PowerAlert style control path; the exact implementation must be verified from code and runtime configuration.
- LLDAP at `10.0.20.101` has been used as the authentication directory for this environment; verify whether and how VM154 currently uses it.
- Current UI capabilities to preserve include outlet state display, outlet naming, individual ON/OFF/REBOOT actions, batch selection/actions, presets, protection/lockout behavior, recent-action logging, configuration save/upload behavior, and a mobile-usable web UI. Verify each capability against the live service before documenting it as implemented.
- Critical devices must be protected from casual shutdown/reboot. In particular, historical design intent protects `MIAM-00133` from dangerous power actions. Verify the current protection model from live configuration/code.
- KVM-related naming has recently been updated. The expected current naming includes:
  - `MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console`
  - `MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM`
  Verify the live configuration and do not reintroduce deprecated KVM labels.

## 4. Non-negotiable safety rules

The capture task is **read-only against production behavior** except for creating temporary capture files and, later, making explicitly reviewed source-control changes in a separate workspace.

The agent MUST NOT during capture or validation:

- send PDU `OFF`, `ON`, `REBOOT`, `CYCLE`, or equivalent actuation commands;
- click production power-control buttons as a test;
- reboot or shut down VM154 or `MIAM-00133`;
- change production outlet names, presets, lockouts, protection flags, credentials, authentication settings, firewall rules, or network configuration;
- rotate secrets;
- edit the running service in place merely to make it easier to capture;
- overwrite production configuration with repository files;
- commit credentials, passwords, tokens, private keys, session secrets, TLS private keys, LLDAP bind credentials, or PDU credentials;
- merge its own pull request.

If any action is required that could alter power state, infrastructure, permissions, secrets, or production data, STOP and request human approval.

## 5. Repository rules

Before making repository changes, read:

1. `README.md`
2. `AGENTS.md`
3. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
6. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
7. `docs/adr/`
8. `docs/tdr/`

Follow the repository's human-approval model.

If no issue/ticket already exists for this work, create one before committing so branch and commit naming can comply with repository policy.

Suggested issue title:

`Capture live PDU Manager source and establish reproducible deployment baseline`

Suggested branch after the issue number is known:

`chore/<ISSUE>-capture-live-pdu-manager`

Suggested commit subject after the issue number is known:

`chore(capture): capture live PDU manager baseline (#<ISSUE>)`

Do not commit directly to `main`.

## 6. Phase A - Establish a production inventory without changing production

From the `MIAM-00133` root shell, begin by proving VM154 exists and is running:

```bash
qm status 154
qm config 154
```

Use the QEMU guest agent where available:

```bash
qm guest exec 154 -- bash -lc 'hostname; date; uname -a; cat /etc/os-release'
```

Capture the output into the work log.

### 6.1 Discover the serving process

Inside VM154, gather:

```bash
systemctl list-units --type=service --all --no-pager
systemctl list-unit-files --type=service --no-pager
ps auxww
ss -lntup
```

Identify the process listening on TCP 443 and any internal application port. For each candidate process, capture:

- PID;
- command line;
- executable path;
- working directory;
- user/group;
- parent process;
- systemd unit or container name;
- environment-file references;
- restart policy;
- log destination.

Useful read-only commands include:

```bash
readlink -f /proc/<PID>/exe
readlink -f /proc/<PID>/cwd
tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/<PID>/cmdline
systemctl status <unit> --no-pager
systemctl cat <unit>
systemctl show <unit>
```

Do not copy `/proc/<PID>/environ` into the repository. It may contain secrets.

### 6.2 Determine the runtime model

Explicitly determine whether the live service uses any of the following:

- Python / Flask / FastAPI / Gunicorn / Uvicorn;
- Node.js;
- Docker / Docker Compose;
- nginx / Apache / Caddy / another reverse proxy;
- a standalone HTTPS development server;
- SQLite / JSON / YAML / flat-file configuration;
- systemd only;
- cron or systemd timers;
- custom shell wrappers.

Record facts only. Do not migrate to a different runtime during capture.

### 6.3 Find live files, not backups

The earlier KVM-label search revealed many historical copies under `/root`, including backup directories and bootstrap scripts. That is evidence that broad string searches are not sufficient to identify the live application.

The agent must derive the live code path from the running process/service/container first. Then inspect only that live path and its declared dependencies.

For every discovered file/directory, classify it as one of:

- **SOURCE** - application code that belongs in Git;
- **DEPLOYMENT** - service units, reverse-proxy config, installer/bootstrap logic;
- **SAFE CONFIG** - non-secret defaults or site mapping appropriate for Git;
- **SECRET CONFIG** - credentials/keys/tokens; never commit;
- **PERSISTENT STATE** - logs, runtime state, mutable uploaded configuration, databases;
- **GENERATED** - caches, bytecode, build output;
- **BACKUP/LEGACY** - historical copies not used by the live service;
- **UNKNOWN** - requires human review.

### 6.4 Capture service/dependency metadata

Capture, when applicable:

- systemd unit files and drop-ins;
- reverse-proxy configuration;
- application source;
- static assets/templates;
- dependency manifests (`requirements.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, lockfiles, etc.);
- exact runtime versions;
- package/service prerequisites;
- file ownership and permissions needed to run;
- listener ports;
- application health behavior;
- log locations;
- persistent data/config locations;
- TLS termination location;
- authentication integration;
- PDU integration points;
- startup/restart commands.

If dependency manifests do not exist, use the live environment as evidence to create a minimal reproducible manifest, but label it as reconstructed and validate it later on a clean staging VM.

## 7. Phase B - Create a clean capture workspace

Create a workspace **outside the production application directory**. Prefer an agent workstation or a temporary directory on `MIAM-00133`, for example:

```text
/root/pdu-manager-capture-<timestamp>/
```

Do not edit live files.

Transfer only the classified SOURCE, DEPLOYMENT, and SAFE CONFIG files into this workspace.

If SSH/SCP to VM154 is available, use a read-only copy path. If not, use a temporary tar archive inside VM154 and transfer it through an available safe mechanism. Avoid printing binary archives or secrets into terminal history when a direct file-copy mechanism is available.

Generate a capture manifest containing:

- source path on VM154;
- destination path in the repository;
- SHA-256 checksum;
- classification;
- whether the file was changed/sanitized before commit;
- reason for exclusion if not committed.

Save the manifest as `docs/CAPTURE_MANIFEST.md` in the branch.

## 8. Phase C - Secret and sensitive-data review

Before `git add`, inspect all captured files for:

- passwords;
- usernames paired with passwords;
- SSH private keys;
- TLS private keys;
- API tokens;
- cookies/session secrets;
- LDAP bind passwords;
- PDU credentials;
- GitHub credentials;
- embedded Basic Auth URLs;
- `.env` files;
- shell history;
- certificates containing private keys;
- database dumps or logs containing sensitive material.

At minimum run a repository secret scanner such as `gitleaks` if available. Also perform targeted text searches.

For every secret found:

1. Leave the production value unchanged on VM154 during capture.
2. Remove the secret from the Git copy.
3. Replace it with environment/config indirection.
4. Add a safe example such as `.env.example` or `config.example.*` containing placeholder values only.
5. Add the real secret-bearing path to `.gitignore` if needed.
6. Document where production will obtain the secret in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` without revealing the value.
7. If a secret was already committed at any point, STOP and request human assistance for rotation/history remediation.

## 9. Phase D - Establish an understandable repository layout

Preserve the application's natural structure when possible. Do not perform a speculative rewrite during capture.

A likely repository shape after discovery may resemble:

```text
/
  README.md
  AGENTS.md
  REQUIREMENTS.md
  FUTURE_WORK.md
  SPRINT.md
  app/ or src/                 # live application source, exact name chosen from discovered stack
  tests/                       # only real tests; do not create fake passing tests
  deploy/
    systemd/                   # captured/reconstructed unit files
    proxy/                     # reverse proxy config if applicable
    scripts/                   # install/deploy/healthcheck scripts
    examples/                  # safe config examples
  docs/
    ARCHITECTURE.md
    OPERATIONS.md
    DEPLOYMENT.md
    CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md
    CAPTURE_MANIFEST.md
    adr/
    tdr/
```

Do not force this layout if the live application already has a clear conventional structure. The objective is reproducibility, not cosmetic reorganization.

## 10. Phase E - Document the observed implementation

Update the project documentation package supplied with this plan using facts discovered from VM154.

Every statement about implementation should be marked one of:

- **Implemented and verified** - directly observed in live code/runtime;
- **Implemented but not yet fully validated** - present but not safely exercised;
- **Required target** - approved behavior that the captured code must satisfy;
- **Future work** - explicitly not current scope;
- **Unknown / requires human confirmation** - evidence is insufficient.

Do not claim a power-control action was validated by actuating production hardware.

## 11. Phase F - Reproduce the service without touching production

Before calling the capture complete, prove the repository contains enough information to recreate the application in a clean environment.

Preferred validation order:

1. static dependency/install validation;
2. application start with mock or disabled PDU backend;
3. HTTPS/web UI load;
4. configuration parsing;
5. authentication integration in a non-production mode where practical;
6. read-only status path using mocks or a read-only adapter;
7. no-actuation smoke tests.

Use a disposable staging VM/container rather than VM154 for destructive setup testing.

If a clean deployment cannot be achieved from the repository, do **not** hide the gap. Record every missing dependency/manual step in `docs/CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md` and continue improving the captured source until a clean deployment is possible or a human decides to accept temporary technical debt.

## 12. Phase G - Git commit and pull request

Before commit:

```bash
git status
git diff --check
```

Run every project validation command that now exists. Record exact results.

Commit only after:

- live source path is proven from runtime evidence;
- secret scan passes;
- historical backups are excluded;
- generated files/logs are excluded;
- documentation reflects the actual captured stack;
- a clean/staging start has been attempted and results recorded.

Push the feature branch to:

`startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`

Open a pull request using the repository template.

The PR must explicitly state:

- live service paths discovered;
- runtime stack discovered;
- systemd/container/proxy units discovered;
- files captured;
- files intentionally excluded;
- secret remediation performed;
- clean deployment test result;
- known limitations;
- requirements covered;
- ADR/TDR changes;
- exact validation performed;
- whether production VM154 was changed (**expected: no functional production change during capture**).

**STOP after PR creation. A human must approve the merge.**

## 13. Completion criteria

The capture task is complete only when all of the following are true:

- [ ] The exact process serving `https://10.0.20.154/` has been identified.
- [ ] The exact live application directory has been identified from runtime evidence.
- [ ] Source code has been copied from the live deployment without relying on old backup folders.
- [ ] Runtime/service/proxy/dependency definitions are in Git or documented reproducibly.
- [ ] Secrets are excluded and externalized.
- [ ] The repository documents business purpose, requirements, architecture, operations, deployment, limitations, and future work.
- [ ] A clean/staging deployment has been attempted from source.
- [ ] Validation results are recorded honestly.
- [ ] No production power actuation occurred during capture/validation.
- [ ] No production infrastructure reboot was performed for capture.
- [ ] The work is committed on a branch and pushed.
- [ ] A pull request exists.
- [ ] The agent has NOT merged its own PR.

## 14. Required agent handoff

At completion, write `docs/handoffs/<date>-source-capture-handoff.md` or provide an equivalent PR handoff containing:

1. issue and branch;
2. commit SHA(s);
3. PR URL;
4. live source path;
5. service unit/process name;
6. runtime and dependency versions;
7. external dependencies;
8. secret/config strategy;
9. staging reproduction result;
10. validation commands/results;
11. risks and limitations;
12. ADR/TDR status;
13. future work discovered;
14. exact next action for the human reviewer.

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

## 12. PDU Manager production safety additions

This application can control real electrical power. Treat production interactions as high-consequence infrastructure operations.

Agents MUST NOT perform real PDU ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE operations as tests unless a human explicitly authorizes the exact outlet/action for that session.

During normal development, source capture, CI, staging, or deployment verification:

- use mocks/fixtures for actuation behavior;
- prefer read-only outlet-state checks when technically enforceable;
- do not reboot VM154 or `MIAM-00133` merely to apply application changes;
- restart only the application service(s) when a service restart is sufficient;
- do not modify production outlet labels, presets, protection/lockout settings, or credentials as an incidental test;
- never place PDU/LDAP/TLS credentials in Git;
- preserve protection of critical power targets;
- treat any change that could remove a safety interlock as security/safety sensitive and stop for human review.

The production endpoint is currently `https://10.0.20.154/` on VM154 hosted by `MIAM-00133`. This location is an approved deployment target, not permission for unrestricted infrastructure changes.

Acceptance criteria live directly with each requirement.\n\n## Business goals\n\nThe PDU Manager shall reduce operational friction and risk when managing rack power in MARION-IA-USA by providing a centralized, human-readable, auditable, and recoverable interface to network-managed PDUs.\n\nKey business outcomes are:\n\n- fewer trips to physically access rack equipment;\n- lower risk of powering off the wrong device;\n- faster troubleshooting and recovery;\n- consistent asset/outlet naming aligned with server architecture records;\n- traceable operational actions;\n- recoverability if VM154 is lost;\n- safe, reviewable changes through Git and CI/CD rather than ad-hoc production edits.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: PDU Inventory and Human-Readable Mapping\n\n### REQ-001 - Present configured PDUs in one interface\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall present the configured network-managed PDUs through one browser-accessible interface.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- The operator can distinguish each configured PDU.\n- Each PDU displays its configured identity and address/role without exposing credentials.\n- Failure of one PDU does not make the identity of the other configured PDUs ambiguous.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- UI/integration test with mock PDU inventory.\n- Read-only production comparison after deployment.\n\n### REQ-002 - Display human-readable outlet identity\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall display each outlet with a stable outlet number and a human-readable asset/load label.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Outlet number remains visible even when a custom label exists.\n- Empty/unassigned outlets are distinguishable from named assets.\n- Displayed labels are loaded from controlled configuration rather than hard-coded in presentation logic where practical.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Unit/configuration test.\n- UI comparison against approved architecture mapping.\n\n### REQ-003 - Preserve approved KVM naming\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall use the current approved KVM-related asset names in production configuration.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- The JetKVM asset is labeled `MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console` where applicable.\n- The TESmart KVM asset is labeled `MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM` where applicable.\n- Deprecated KVM naming is not reintroduced by deployment defaults or seed configuration.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Configuration test and read-only UI inspection.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Outlet State and Power Control\n\n### REQ-004 - Display outlet power state\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall display the latest available power state for each managed outlet.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- The UI differentiates at least ON, OFF, and unknown/error state.\n- Refreshing state does not actuate an outlet.\n- Backend failure is surfaced rather than silently represented as a successful state.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Mock-backend integration tests.\n- Read-only state retrieval test where safely supported.\n\n### REQ-005 - Support deliberate individual outlet actions\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall support deliberate operator-requested ON, OFF, and REBOOT/CYCLE actions for outlets that are not blocked by safety policy.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- The requested outlet and requested action are unambiguous before execution.\n- The application reports success/failure returned by the backend.\n- A page refresh or state query never implicitly executes a power action.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Automated tests use a mock PDU backend only.\n- Real hardware actuation is manual and separately authorized, not part of CI.\n\n### REQ-006 - Support selected/batch power actions\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall allow an operator to select multiple permitted outlets and intentionally issue one supported action to that selection.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- The current selection is visible before action execution.\n- The operator can clear/select the selection without changing power state.\n- Per-outlet results are visible when a batch action contains mixed outcomes.\n- Protected outlets remain protected during batch actions.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Mock-backend integration test.\n\n### REQ-007 - Support operational presets\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall support named presets/groupings for recurring operational selections/actions as configured by authorized operators.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Preset membership is inspectable.\n- Applying/selecting a preset does not itself perform an unconfirmed dangerous power action unless explicitly designed and approved to do so.\n- Protected outlets cannot be bypassed merely by being included in a preset.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Unit/integration tests against mock configuration/backend.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Safety and Protection\n\n### REQ-008 - Protect critical outlets\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall support protection/lockout rules that prevent dangerous actions against designated critical outlets unless an explicitly authorized override path is used.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Protected outlets are visibly distinguishable.\n- Normal OFF and REBOOT/CYCLE paths cannot bypass protection.\n- Batch actions respect protection.\n- The production mapping protects critical infrastructure according to current operator policy, including the protection intent for `MIAM-00133` where its power path is represented.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Automated policy tests using mocks/config fixtures.\n- Read-only inspection of production protection configuration.\n\n### REQ-009 - Fail safe when backend state is uncertain\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall not represent an unverified power action as successful when communication with a PDU fails or returns an ambiguous result.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Communication errors are visible to the operator.\n- Ambiguous results are not converted to success.\n- The UI permits a subsequent read-only refresh/reconciliation.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Fault-injection tests against the mock backend.\n\n### REQ-010 - Prevent automated production actuation during CI/deployment validation\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe project shall provide a validation mode/path in which automated CI, staging, and production health checks cannot send real outlet actuation commands.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- PR CI requires no production PDU actuation credentials.\n- Staging defaults to a mock/non-actuating backend.\n- Production deployment health checks use only application/read-only checks.\n- No deployment workflow issues ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE as a smoke test.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- CI workflow review and tests.\n- Deployment-script static review.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Authentication and Access Control\n\n### REQ-011 - Authenticate operator access\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall require the configured authentication mechanism for operator access to protected PDU Manager functions.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Unauthenticated users cannot execute power-control actions.\n- Authentication failure does not expose secrets.\n- Current LLDAP integration at `10.0.20.101`, if present in the live service, is documented and reproducible without committing bind credentials.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Authentication integration tests where safe.\n- Source/runtime inspection during capture.\n\n### REQ-012 - Keep credentials outside source control\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product and deployment shall keep PDU, LDAP, session, TLS-private-key, and deployment credentials outside Git.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Repository secret scanning passes.\n- Example configuration contains placeholders only.\n- Deployment documentation specifies secret names/locations without values.\n- Runtime secrets are readable only by the required service/deployment identities.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Secret scan and permissions review.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Logging, Auditability, and Diagnostics\n\n### REQ-013 - Record operator power actions\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall record enough information about power actions to support troubleshooting and accountability.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Log entry includes timestamp, target outlet/device, requested action, and outcome.\n- Authentication identity is recorded where available and appropriate.\n- Logs do not expose passwords, private keys, or session secrets.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Mock-action log test.\n- Log redaction review.\n\n### REQ-014 - Provide operator-visible recent-action feedback\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall provide recent-action/result feedback in the web interface or an equivalently accessible operational view.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Recent success/failure outcomes are visible without shell access.\n- A failed backend operation is distinguishable from a successful one.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- UI/integration test.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Configuration Management\n\n### REQ-015 - Preserve configuration across application deployments\n\n**Requirement**\n\nDeploying new application code shall not silently overwrite the production outlet mapping, presets, protection settings, or other mutable site configuration.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Mutable site configuration is stored outside immutable code release directories or otherwise protected from overwrite.\n- Deployment creates a recoverable backup before any compatible configuration migration.\n- Rollback does not unexpectedly restore stale site mappings.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Staging upgrade/rollback test.\n\n### REQ-016 - Support configuration export/import behavior that is documented and validated\n\n**Requirement**\n\nIf the production product exposes configuration save/upload functionality, the repository shall document its format, validation rules, and recovery behavior.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Export does not include secrets unless explicitly designed, encrypted, and approved.\n- Import validates structure before applying changes.\n- Invalid configuration does not leave the application in a partially applied state.\n- The exact currently implemented `.md` configuration workflow shown by the production UI is verified during source capture before being described as fully implemented.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Source inspection and staging tests.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Web User Experience\n\n### REQ-017 - Provide browser-based management UI\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe product shall provide a browser-based interface suitable for routine desktop operation and practical mobile use.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Primary PDU/outlet controls are usable without direct shell access.\n- Core views do not require horizontal layouts that make normal mobile operation impractical.\n- Error and protection states are visible.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- UI review at representative desktop/mobile widths.\n\n### REQ-018 - Expose production service over HTTPS\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe production web interface shall be served over HTTPS on the management network.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- `https://10.0.20.154/` or its approved successor responds over HTTPS.\n- TLS private keys are not stored in Git.\n- Certificate strategy is documented in deployment documentation.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- HTTPS health check and configuration review.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Reproducibility and Source Control\n\n### REQ-019 - GitHub shall become the authoritative application source\n\n**Requirement**\n\nAfter parity validation and human approval, the GitHub repository shall be the authoritative source for PDU Manager application code and deployment assets.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Live source has been captured from VM154 and reviewed.\n- Historical backup directories are not mistaken for active source.\n- Every production code release can be traced to a Git commit/release artifact.\n- Ad-hoc production code editing is no longer the normal change path.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Capture manifest, PR review, deployment release metadata.\n\n### REQ-020 - Reproduce the service from a clean environment\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe repository shall contain enough code, dependency definitions, configuration examples, and deployment automation to make a clean supported VM host the PDU Manager without relying on undocumented production state.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- A clean staging VM can be provisioned from repository instructions/automation.\n- Application starts and serves the expected UI.\n- Required external integrations are documented.\n- Manual prerequisites are explicit and minimized.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Clean staging deployment test.\n\n### REQ-021 - Validate changes automatically in CI\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe project shall run automated CI checks on proposed code changes before merge.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Tests/build/static checks appropriate to the discovered stack run on PRs.\n- Secret scanning runs before merge.\n- CI does not require dangerous production PDU access.\n- Failures block the normal merge path.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- GitHub Actions workflow execution.\n\n### REQ-022 - Deploy reviewed releases through controlled CD\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe project shall support deployment of a human-reviewed, staging-validated release artifact to VM154 through a controlled CD process.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Production deployment uses the exact artifact validated in staging.\n- Production deployment requires the configured human approval gate.\n- Deployment restarts only required application services when possible, not the entire VM.\n- Release identity/commit SHA is recorded.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Staging deployment and supervised production deployment.\n\n### REQ-023 - Support rollback to a prior known-good release\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe deployment process shall support restoration of the immediately previous known-good application release when post-deploy health checks fail.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Rollback is tested in staging.\n- Mutable configuration/state is not lost merely because code is rolled back.\n- The rollback procedure is documented and automatable.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Staging rollback exercise.\n\n---\n\n## Feature: Documentation and Agent Operability\n\n### REQ-024 - Maintain current architecture and operations documentation\n\n**Requirement**\n\nThe repository shall document the system sufficiently for another qualified human or AI agent to understand its purpose, boundaries, runtime, deployment, safe operating model, and known limitations without relying on hidden chat history.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- `README.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, and `docs/CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md` reflect the current release.\n- Significant decisions are recorded in ADRs.\n- Accepted/in-progress technical debt is recorded in TDRs.\n- Future ideas remain in `FUTURE_WORK.md` until approved.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- PR documentation review.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Introduction and Goals\n\n### Purpose\n\nThe PDU Manager provides a centralized internal web interface for monitoring and deliberately controlling power outlets on network-managed PDUs in the MARION-IA-USA server environment.\n\nThe service reduces the operational risk of interacting with raw PDU interfaces and provides human-readable mapping, protection, logging, and a recoverable software change path.\n\n### Requirements Overview\n\nArchitecturally significant requirements include:\n\n- `REQ-008` - protect critical outlets;\n- `REQ-010` - automated validation must not actuate production hardware;\n- `REQ-011` / `REQ-012` - authenticated access and externalized secrets;\n- `REQ-015` - code deployment must preserve mutable site configuration;\n- `REQ-018` - production HTTPS;\n- `REQ-019` - GitHub becomes authoritative application source after parity validation;\n- `REQ-020` - clean-environment reproducibility;\n- `REQ-022` / `REQ-023` - controlled production deployment and rollback.\n\nSee `../REQUIREMENTS.md` for the complete requirement set.\n\n### Quality Goals\n\n1. **Safety:** automated testing/deployment cannot unexpectedly change electrical power state.\n2. **Recoverability:** VM154 can be recreated from source, deployment automation, externalized secrets, and backed-up mutable configuration.\n3. **Traceability:** production releases map to reviewed Git commits/artifacts.\n4. **Operability:** operators can understand device identity, state, action result, and protection status from the browser UI.\n5. **Maintainability:** another human/AI agent can continue work from repository-local documentation and code.\n\n### Stakeholders\n\n| Stakeholder / Role | Primary Concern |\n|---|---|\n| Infrastructure operator | Correct device identity, safe power actions, clear status, rapid recovery |\n| Startup Teams engineering | Reproducible source, reviewable changes, tests, maintainable code |\n| AI coding/ops agents | Explicit guardrails, discoverable commands, no hidden production state |\n| System owner | Avoid accidental outages and preserve operational continuity |\n\n## 2. Architecture Constraints\n\n- Production currently runs on VM154 hosted by `MIAM-00133`.\n- Production UI is currently reached at `https://10.0.20.154/`.\n- Managed PDU endpoints exist on the private `10.0.20.x` management network, including `10.0.20.151`, `.152`, and `.153`.\n- Automated CI/deployment validation must not use real outlet actuation.\n- Secrets must not be committed to Git.\n- Human approval is required before merge under the current repository governance model.\n- Production deployment should restart application services rather than rebooting VM154 when a service restart is sufficient.\n- The current runtime stack must be captured before selecting a new deployment technology.\n\n## 3. Context and Scope\n\n### Business Context / C4 System Context\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    Operator[Infrastructure Operator] -->|HTTPS| UI[PDU Manager\\nVM154 / 10.0.20.154]\n    UI -->|Authenticate - verify live integration| LLDAP[LLDAP\\n10.0.20.101]\n    UI -->|PDU control/status\\nexact protocol to verify| PDU151[PDU\\n10.0.20.151]\n    UI -->|PDU control/status\\nexact protocol to verify| PDU152[PDU\\n10.0.20.152]\n    UI -->|PDU control/status\\nexact protocol to verify| PDU153[PDU\\n10.0.20.153]\n    PDU151 --> Loads1[Powered rack equipment]\n    PDU152 --> Loads2[Powered rack equipment]\n    PDU153 --> Loads3[Networking / console / other rack loads]\n    GitHub[GitHub Repository] -->|reviewed release artifact| Deploy[Controlled deployment path]\n    Deploy --> UI\n```\n\n### Technical Context\n\nKnown interfaces:\n\n| Interface | Direction | Purpose | Status |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| HTTPS | Operator -> VM154 | Browser UI | Known current deployment |\n| PDU management protocol | VM154 -> PDUs | Read state and execute approved power actions | **Unknown / verify exact implementation**; UI has described a direct SSH/PowerAlert backend |\n| LDAP/LLDAP | VM154 -> `10.0.20.101` | Authentication | Known environment dependency; **verify exact live implementation** |\n| GitHub | Engineering/CI -> repository | Source/change management | Target source of truth |\n| Deployment runner | CI/CD -> VM154 | Controlled software release | Target, not yet implemented |\n\nTrust boundary: production PDU credentials and directory credentials must remain outside the repository and should be accessible only to the identities that require them.\n\n## 4. Solution Strategy\n\n### Current strategy\n\nThe PDU Manager runs as a single internal web application on VM154 and communicates with the managed PDUs over the management network.\n\nThe exact application framework, reverse proxy, process manager, data format, and service unit are **Unknown / verify from live VM154** during source capture.\n\n### Target strategy\n\n- Capture the live service without rewriting it.\n- Establish GitHub as the authoritative source after parity validation.\n- Separate immutable application code from secrets and mutable site configuration.\n- Test PDU behavior through mocks/non-actuating adapters.\n- Reproduce the service on a clean staging VM.\n- Build immutable release artifacts in CI.\n- Deploy the exact staging-tested artifact to VM154 only after human approval.\n- Maintain a known-good rollback release.\n\n## 5. Building Block View\n\n| Building Block | Responsibility | Interfaces / Dependencies | Code Location |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Web UI | Display PDUs/outlets/state/protection and accept deliberate operator actions | Browser, application backend | **To be captured** |\n| Application controller | Validate requests, enforce protection, coordinate backend operations, produce action results | Web UI, PDU adapter, config, auth, logs | **To be captured** |\n| PDU adapter | Read outlet state and issue explicitly authorized power operations | Managed PDUs | **To be captured** |\n| Authentication integration | Authenticate operators | LLDAP/other current mechanism | **To be captured** |\n| Configuration layer | PDU inventory, outlet labels, presets, protection settings, safe defaults | Persistent config | **To be captured** |\n| Action log | Record operational actions/results without secrets | Application/runtime log store | **To be captured** |\n| Deployment package | Install/start/restart/rollback application reproducibly | OS/systemd/container runtime | Target; not yet complete |\n| CI/CD workflows | Validate source and deliver reviewed releases | GitHub Actions + approved runner | Target; not yet implemented |", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
